Well-formedness of types and environments.

The well-formedness rules omitted from the main paper are found in
Figure

13
. We write

\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau\produces\Gamma
as shorthand for
\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma
and
\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau
.

We first prove that the subtyping relations are transitive.

Lemma 5

1.

If
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
then
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\implies\left\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime}}\right\rrbracket
.

2.

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
and
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}
, then
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}

3.

If
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}

4.

If
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, and
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}
, then
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}
.

5.

If
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, then
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof

1.

It suffices to show that
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\implies[\mathit{x}/\,\nu\,]\,\varphi^{\prime}
for any
\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
where
\Gamma^{\prime}(\mathit{x})=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi^{\prime}}\right\}
.
From
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
we have
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi\implies\varphi^{\prime}
where
\Gamma(\mathit{x})=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}
.
We must then have
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge[\mathit{x}/\,\nu\,]\,\varphi\implies[\mathit{x}/\,\nu\,]\,\varphi^{\prime}
. From the definition of
\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
we have
\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge[\mathit{x}/\,\nu\,]\,\varphi\iff\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
, giving the desired result.

2.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. We only consider the base case where
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\right\}
and
\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\right\}
,
the case for reference types follows from the induction hypothesis.
By further inversion on
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}
we therefore have:

τ
_3 =
{

ν
:
int
∣
φ
_3
}

⊧⟦ Γ⟧ ∧
φ
_1 ⟹
φ
_2

⊧⟦ Γ⟧ ∧
φ
_2 ⟹
φ
_3

From which it is immediate that we must have
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies\varphi_{{\mathrm{3}}}
, whereby
S-Int
gives
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}
.

3.

By induction on
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. The case for reference types is immediate from the inductive hypothesis, we focus
on the base case where
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\right\}
and
\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\right\}
, and where
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
From
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
and
Item

1
above, we have
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\implies\left\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime}}\right\rrbracket
from which we can derive
\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, i.e.,

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

4.

Immediate from
Items

2
and
3
.

5.

Immediate corollary of
Item

4
.

Definition 1

A value
v
reaches an integer with
n
dereferences in heap
\mathit{H}
when it is in the relation
\mathit{H}\vdash v\Downarrow n

defined as the smallest relation closed under the following rules:

1.

If
v\in\mathbb{Z}
then
\mathit{H}\vdash v\Downarrow 0

2.

If
\mathit{H}\vdash v\Downarrow n
and
\mathit{H}(\mathit{a})\,=\,v
then
\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{a}\Downarrow n+1

We will write
\mathit{H}\vdash v\Downarrow|\tau|
to indicate a value
v
is shape consistent with
\tau
in heap
\mathit{H}
, where

|\tau|
is the number of reference constructors in the type
\tau
.

We also prove a standard inversion lemma to
handle the fact our typing rules are not syntax directed.

Lemma 6
(Inversion)

If

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
,
then there exists some
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}
,
\tau_{\mathit{p}}
, and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

such that
\Gamma\leq\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}
,
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}},\tau_{\mathit{p}}\leq\Gamma^{\prime},\tau
, and:

1.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathit{x}
then
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{x})=\tau_{\mathit{p}}+\tau^{\prime}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}=\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}]
.

2.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e}
, then

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{x}],\mathit{x}:(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{x}}\mathit{x}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{y})\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

and
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}})
where
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y})=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

3.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\LET\mathit{x}=n\IN\mathit{e}
then
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}},\mathit{x}:\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,n}\right\}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}
and
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}})
.

4.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\IFZERO\mathit{x}\THEN\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\ELSE\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
then:

•

\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{x})=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}

•

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\wedge\nu\,=\,0}\right\}]\vdash\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

•

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\wedge\nu\,\neq\,0}\right\}]\vdash\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

5.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\LET\mathit{x}=\MKREF\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e}
, then
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y})=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
,
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}],\mathit{x}:(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{x}}\mathit{x}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{y})\TREF^{1}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}
, and
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}})

6.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\LET\mathit{x}=*\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e}
, then:

•

\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y})=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r}

•

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime\prime}\TREF^{r}],\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

•

\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}})

•

\tau^{\prime\prime}=\begin{cases}(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{x})&r>0\\
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}&r=0\end{cases}

7.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{f}^{\ell}(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}})\IN\mathit{e}
then:

•

\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}})=\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{i}}
for each
i\in\left\{{1,\ldots,n}\right\}

•

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}}\hookleftarrow\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}],\mathit{x}:\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

•

\Theta(\mathit{f})=\forall\lambda.\left\langle{\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}},\dots,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\tau_{\mathit{n}}}\right\rangle\ra\left\langle{\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\dots,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}\mid\tau}\right\rangle

•

\sigma_{\alpha}=[\ell:\mathcal{L}/\lambda]

•

\sigma_{x}=[\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\cdots[\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}]

•

\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}})

8.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathit{y}\WRITE\mathit{x}\SEQ\mathit{e}
then:

•

\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{x})=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}

•

\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y})=\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{1}

•

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}][\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{x})\TREF^{1}]\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

•

The shapes of
\tau^{\prime}
and
\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
are similar, i.e,
|\tau^{\prime}|\,=\,|\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}|
.

9.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathbf{alias}(\mathit{x}=\mathit{y})\SEQ\mathit{e}
then there exist some
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}},\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},r_{{\mathrm{1}}},r_{{\mathrm{2}}},r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}
such that:

•

\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\approx\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

•

\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{x})=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y})=\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

•

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}][\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

10.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathbf{alias}(\mathit{x}=*\mathit{y})\SEQ\mathit{e}
then there exist some
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}},\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},r_{{\mathrm{1}}},r_{{\mathrm{2}}},r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},r
, such that:

•

\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\approx\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

•

\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{x})=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y})=(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\TREF^{r}

•

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}][\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\TREF^{r}]\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

11.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\SEQ\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
then
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}\vdash\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

12.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathit{x}\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime}}
then
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}:\tau^{\prime}+\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}]\vdash\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}]
and
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}]\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

13.

If
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathbf{assert}(\varphi)\SEQ\mathit{e}
then
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}\models\varphi
and
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

Proof

By straightforward induction on the typing relation and
the transitivity of the subtyping relation
Lemma

5
.

The only case of note is the case for
\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathit{x}\SEQ\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
. If the subderivation for
\mathit{x}

has applications of
T-Sub
then the subtypings on the output environment can be pushed
into application subtyping on input environments when typing
\mathit{e^{\prime}}
. Similarly,
any input subtypings on the input environment of the derivation of
\mathit{x}
can be pushed
into
T-Sub
rules such that
\Gamma\leq\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}:\tau^{\prime}+\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}]
.

Lemmas

7
and
8
prove some standard properties of execution contexts:
any decomposition of a well-typed expression into a execution context and redex can be well-typed,
and substituting a well-typed expression matching a context’s hole type yields a well-typed expression

Lemma 7

For any
\mathit{E}
and
\mathit{e^{\prime}}
such that
\mathit{E}[\mathit{e^{\prime}}]=\mathit{e}
where

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
there exists some
\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}
,
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
such that

\Theta\mid[]:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\mid\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
and

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.

Proof

By induction on the structure of
\mathit{E}
.

Case
\mathit{E}=[]
:

Trivial, by taking
\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}=\tau
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}=\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Case
\mathit{E}=\mathit{E^{\prime}}\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}
:

Then
\mathit{E}[\mathit{e^{\prime}}]=\mathit{E^{\prime}}[\mathit{e^{\prime}}]\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}=\mathit{e}
. By

Lemma

6
we have

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}\vdash\mathit{E^{\prime}}[\mathit{e^{\prime}}]:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}&\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}\\
\Gamma\leq\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}&\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}},\tau_{\mathit{p}}\leq\Gamma^{\prime},\tau\end{array}

for some
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}
, and
\tau_{\mathit{p}}
.

By the induction hypothesis
we then have
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
and

\Theta\mid[]:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\mid\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E^{\prime}}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
. for some
\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.

Next, as
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}},\tau_{\mathit{p}}\leq\Gamma^{\prime},\tau
by an application of
T-Sub
,
we have
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
. By
TE-Seq
, we therefore
have:
\Theta\mid[]:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\mid\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E^{\prime}}\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Finally, from
\Gamma\leq\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}
and
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
,
and application of
T-Sub
, we have
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.

Lemma 8

If
\Theta\mid[]:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E}:\tau^{\prime\prime}\produces\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
and
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
for some
\Gamma
,
then
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{E}[\mathit{e}]:\tau^{\prime\prime}\produces\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof

By induction on the typing derivation of
\mathit{E}
.

Case
TE-Seq
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{E}=\mathit{E^{\prime}}\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime}}\\
\mathit{E}[\mathit{e}]=\mathit{E^{\prime}}[\mathit{e}]\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime}}\\
\Theta\mid[]:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E^{\prime}}:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\\
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau^{\prime\prime}\produces\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\\
\end{array}

By the induction hypothesis we have
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{E^{\prime}}[\mathit{e}]:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
. We then
have our result via an application of
T-Seq
.

Case
TE-Hole
:

Trivial, as
\tau=\tau^{\prime\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathit{E}[\mathit{e}]=\mathit{e}
.

Lemma 9
(Context Variable Substitution)

1.

If
\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
then
[\mathcal{L}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}=[\mathcal{L}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+[\mathcal{L}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}

2.

For any
\vec{\ell}
:

(a)

If
\lambda\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma
then
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma

(b)

If
\lambda\mid\Gamma\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau
then
\vec{\ell}\mid[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau

(c)

If
\lambda\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau\produces\Gamma
then
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau\produces[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma

3.

For any
\Gamma
,
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
,
\lambda
and
\vec{\ell}
, If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma\vdash[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}

4.

If
\Gamma\models\varphi
where
\lambda\not\in\mathbf{FCV}\,(\varphi)
then
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma\models\varphi

5.

If
\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
then
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau\produces[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma^{\prime}

Proof

1.

By straightforward induction on the definition of
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}
.

2.

Observe that any substitution of context variables cannot change simple
types within
\Gamma
and thus all types and refinements remain well-formed with respect
to integer variables in
\Gamma
.
It thus suffices to show that
\mathbf{FCV}\,([\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\varphi)\subseteq\mathbf{CV}\,(\vec{\ell})=\emptyset

for any refinement
\varphi
appearing in
\tau
or a type in
\Gamma
.
By the assumed well-formedness of
\tau
with respect to context
variable
\lambda
(resp.
\Gamma
), after substitution all free
context variables in
\tau
(resp. the types in
\Gamma
) will be
replaced with
\vec{\ell}
. Thus, post-substitution no free context
variables appear in the refinement of
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau
(resp. refinements of
types in
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma
), trivially satisfying our requirements.

3.

If
\lambda
does not appear free in
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
or
\Gamma
, then the result trivially holds. Let us then assume

\lambda
appears free. We prove the result by induction on the subtyping derivation.

Case
S-Ref
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}&\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\\
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=([\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}&[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=([\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\\
\Gamma\vdash\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}&r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\end{array}

We must show that
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma\vdash[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

which holds immediately from the induction hypothesis.

Case
S-Int
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\right\}&\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\right\}\\
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\right\}&[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\right\}\\
\Gamma\models\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}\end{array}

We must show that
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma\models[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, i.e.
\models\left\llbracket{[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
From our assumption that
\Gamma\models\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
we have that
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is valid,
whereby the formula
\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is true for any possible concrete valuation of the free context
variable
\lambda
. As
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
is equivalent to
\left\llbracket{[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma}\right\rrbracket

we have the formula
\left\llbracket{[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
must also be valid.

4.

If
\lambda
does not appear free in
\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
, then the result trivially holds. Otherwise

\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\implies\varphi
holds for any concrete valuation of the free context variable
\lambda
.
Then the formula
\models\left\llbracket{[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\implies\varphi
must be valid from the equivalence of
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
and
\left\llbracket{[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
.

5.

By induction on the typing derivation
\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
.
In every case, that
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau\produces[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma

holds from
Item

2
.

Case
T-Var
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e}=\mathit{x}&\tau=\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\\
\Gamma=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}[\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}]&\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}]\end{array}

By application of
Item

1
.

Case
T-LetInt
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e}=\LET\mathit{x}=n\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}&\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,n}\right\}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}\\
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})&\end{array}

The induction hypothesis gives

\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,n}\right\}\vdash\mathit{e}:[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau\produces[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma^{\prime}

We conclude
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma\vdash\LET\mathit{x}=n\IN\mathit{e}:[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau\produces[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma^{\prime}

as required.

Case
T-Let
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}&\mathit{e}=\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}&\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})\\
&\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}&\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\Gamma[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{x})],\mathit{x}:(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{x}}\mathit{x}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{y})\\
&\Gamma\quad(\mathit{y})=\mathit{y}\COL\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\end{array}

By
Item

1
,

([\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma)(\mathit{y})=[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})=([\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
. We must then
show that
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau\produces[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma^{\prime}

where

\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=([\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma)[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}\,=\,\mathit{x}],\mathit{x}:([\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{x}}\mathit{x}\,=\,\mathit{y})

As
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
the induction hypothesis gives the required typing judgment.

Case
T-If,T-Seq
:

By trivial application of the inductive hypothesis.

Case
T-MkRef,T-Deref
:

By reasoning similar to
T-Let
.

Case
T-Call
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e}=\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{f}^{\ell}(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}})\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}\\
\sigma_{x}=[\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\cdots[\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}]\\
\sigma_{\alpha}=[\ell:\lambda/\lambda^{\prime}]\\
\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}\\
\mathit{y}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})\\
\Theta(\mathit{f})=\forall\lambda^{\prime}.\left\langle{\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}},\dots,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\tau_{\mathit{n}}}\right\rangle\ra\left\langle{\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\dots,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}\mid\tau^{\prime}}\right\rangle\\
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\Gamma[\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}}\hookleftarrow\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}],\mathit{x}:\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau^{\prime}\end{array}

We must first show that for
\sigma_{\alpha}^{\prime}=[\ell:\vec{\ell}/\lambda^{\prime}]
:

\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau\produces[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma^{\prime}

where
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}}=([\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma)[\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}}\hookleftarrow\sigma_{\alpha}^{\prime}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}],\mathit{x}:\sigma_{\alpha}^{\prime}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau^{\prime}
.

We first observe that
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}}=[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
(this follows from the
equivalence of
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,[\ell:\lambda/\lambda^{\prime}]
and

[\ell:\vec{\ell}/\lambda^{\prime}]
) whereby the induction hypothesis
gives the required typing derivation.

We must also show that

\forall i\in\left\{{1..n}\right\}.([\ell:\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma)(\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}})=\sigma_{\alpha}^{\prime}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{i}}
.
From the assumed well-typing of the term under
\lambda
we have
that
\forall i\in\left\{{1..n}\right\}.\Gamma(\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}})=\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{i}}
. Recall
that
\sigma_{\alpha}^{\prime}
is equivalent to
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\sigma_{\alpha}
, whereby we have

[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma(\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}})=[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{i}}=\sigma_{\alpha}^{\prime}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{i}}
for any
\mathit{i}
as
equality is preserved by consistent substitution.

Case
T-Assign
:

By the inductive hypothesis and application of
Item

1
.

Case
T-Alias
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\Gamma[\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}][\mathit{y}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\vdash\mathbf{alias}(\mathit{x}=\mathit{y})\SEQ\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma\\
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\approx\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\\
\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\Gamma[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}][\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma\end{array}

From
Item

1
we have that
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}})+[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}})

and similarly for
\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
. It therefore remains to show that:

[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\approx[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}})

For which it suffices to show that
\bullet\vdash[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\leq[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}})

and
\bullet\vdash[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\leq[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
. From

\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\approx\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
these both follow from
Item

3
,
whereby the result follows from the inductive hypothesis.

Case
T-AliasPtr
:

By similar reasoning to the
T-Alias
case.

Case
T-Sub
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}&\Gamma\leq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\\
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\Gamma^{\prime},\tau\\
\end{array}

By the induction hypothesis we have that:
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{e}:[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
If we show that
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma\leq[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma^{\prime},[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau

we will have the required result. To show the first requirement, for any
\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)
we have that

[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma\vdash[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma(\mathit{x})\leq[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}(\mathit{x})
from
Item

3
so we have
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma\leq[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.
To show the latter requirement, we observe that
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma^{\prime},[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\,\tau
is equivalent to showing

[\vec{\ell}/\lambda](\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})\leq[\vec{\ell}/\lambda](\Gamma^{\prime},\mathit{x}:\tau)
for some
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})
,
whereby we have the required subtyping relationship from the application of
Item

3
.

Case
T-Assert
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{assert}(\varphi)\SEQ\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}&\Gamma\models\varphi\\
\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}&\epsilon\mid\Gamma\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\varphi\end{array}

By induction hypothesis, the result holds if we can show
[\vec{\ell}/\lambda]\Gamma\models\varphi
which
follows from
Item

4
(that
\lambda\not\in\mathbf{FCV}\,(\varphi)
follows
from the well-formedness of
\varphi
with respect to
\epsilon
).

Lemma 10
(Substitution)

If
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\mathit{x^{\prime}}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)
, then

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\,\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\,\tau\produces[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\,\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Proof

By straightforward induction of typing rules.

We now prove that if every variable satisfies its refinement in a type environment
\Gamma
,
we must have
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
.

Lemma 11

If
\mathbf{SAT}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
then
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
.

Proof

To show
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
, it suffices to show that for any
\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)
where
\Gamma(\mathit{x})=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}

\models[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{x}/\,\nu\,]\,\varphi
holds. From
\mathbf{SAT}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, we must have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\Gamma(\mathit{x}))
, whereby
we have
\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\in\mathbb{Z}
and
[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})/\nu]\varphi
. As
[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{x}/\,\nu\,]\,\varphi
is equivalent to
[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})/\nu]\varphi
,
and we have the desired result.

We prove that subtyping preserves the consistency relation in the following sense.

Lemma 12

If
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
then:

1.

For any
\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
,
\forall\,\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\Gamma(\mathit{x}))(\mathit{a})\leq\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\Gamma^{\prime}(\mathit{x}))(\mathit{a})

2.

\forall\,\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime})(\mathit{a})\leq 1

3.

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau\leq\tau^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
then
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau^{\prime})

4.

\mathbf{SAT}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime})

5.

\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime})

Proof

1.

By induction on
\Gamma\vdash\Gamma(\mathit{x})\leq\Gamma^{\prime}(\mathit{x})
.

2.

Direct consequence of 1 and that
\forall\,\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)(\mathit{a})\leq 1
from
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
.

3.

From
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
we have
\mathbf{SAT}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
which by
Lemma

11
we have
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
.
We now proceed by induction on
\Gamma\vdash\tau\leq\tau^{\prime}
.

Case:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\tau=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}&\tau^{\prime}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi^{\prime}}\right\}\\
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi\implies\varphi^{\prime}\end{array}

From
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
we have
\models[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\varphi
.
We must show that
\models[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\varphi^{\prime}
.
From
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi\implies\varphi^{\prime}
we must have

\models[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\varphi\implies[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\varphi^{\prime}
is valid.
As
\nu
does not appear free in
\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
, we have

\models[\mathit{R}]\,\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\varphi\implies[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\varphi^{\prime}
is
valid whereby the result is immediate.

Case:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\tau=\tau_{\mathit{p}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}&\tau^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\\
r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\end{array}

Immediate from the induction hypothesis.

4.

Immediate consequence of 3 and that

\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
implies that
\Gamma\vdash\Gamma(\mathit{x})\leq\Gamma^{\prime}(\mathit{x})
for any
\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
.

5.

Immediate from 2 and 4.

To show consistency is preserved during evaluation,
Lemmas

13
and
14

show types equivalent according to
\approx
are equivalent for the purposes
of
\mathbf{own}
and
\mathbf{SATv}
. Then
Lemmas

15
and
16
show that the
type addition operator
+
“distributes” over
\mathbf{SATv}
and
\mathbf{own}
.

Lemma 13
(Type Equivalence Preserves Satisfiability)

If
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\approx\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})\iff\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.

Proof

We prove the forward case by induction on
\bullet\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
as implied by

\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\approx\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. The inductive case follows from the IH. In the the base case
where
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\right\}
and
\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\right\}
, from
\bullet\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}

we have that
\models\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is valid, from which we must have
\models[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
,
where from the definition of
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
we must then have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.

The backwards case follows similar reasoning by induction on
\bullet\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}
.

Lemma 14

If
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\approx\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.

Proof

By reasoning similar to that in
Lemma

13
.

Lemma 15

If
\tau_{\mathit{p}}=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, then
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{p}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.

Proof

By induction on the rules used to derive
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\tau_{\mathit{p}}
.

Case
Tadd-Int
:

We have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{p}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
, where
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\right\}
,

\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
, where
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\right\},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\right\}
.

From the definition of ownership, we have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{p}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset
.
It is thus trivial that
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{p}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.

Case
Tadd-Ref
:

We assume
\mathit{v}\,=\,\mathit{a}
and
\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})
, otherwise the result trivially holds.

We have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{p}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
, where
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=(\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}+r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
,
and
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
,
\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
.

From the definition of ownership, we have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{p}})=\{\mathit{a}\mapsto r_{{\mathrm{1}}}+r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{v}),\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
and:

\displaystyle\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})

\displaystyle=\{\mathit{a}\mapsto r_{{\mathrm{1}}}\}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{v}),\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})+\{\mathit{a}\mapsto r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{v}),\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})

\displaystyle=\{\mathit{a}\mapsto r_{{\mathrm{1}}}+r_{{\mathrm{2}}}\}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{v}),\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{v}),\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})

By the induction hypothesis, have that
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{v}),\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{v}),\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{v}),\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
and can conclude that
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{p}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.

Lemma 16

If
\tau_{\mathit{p}}=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, we have

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{\mathit{p}})
iff.
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})

Proof

By induction on the rules used to derive
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. In the following
we only prove the forward direction of the implication; the backwards
direction is symmetric.

Case
Tadd-Int
:

We have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
, where
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\right\}
,
we must show
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
,
where
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\right\},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\right\}
.

From the definition of
\mathbf{SATv}
, we must show
[\mathit{R}]\,[v/\nu]\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
From
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
we have
[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\mathit{x}](\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.
It is immediate that for any value
v
such that
[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu](\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}})
, we must have
[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
We then conclude
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
implies
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.

Case
Tadd-Ref
:

Immediate from the definition of
\mathbf{SATv}
and the inductive hypothesis.

Definition 2

The valid substitution relation, written
\mathit{R}\vdash_{vs}\tau
is the smallest relation closed
under the following rules:

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\forall\,\mathit{x}\in\mathbf{FPV}\,(\varphi)\setminus\left\{{\nu}\right\}.\exists\,n.\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})=n\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathit{R}\vdash_{vs}\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}\end{array}}

\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathit{R}\vdash_{vs}\tau\end{array}}{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathit{R}\vdash_{vs}\tau\TREF^{r}\end{array}}

Lemma 17

If
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma
and
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, then
\forall\,\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma).\mathit{R}\vdash_{vs}\Gamma(\mathit{x})
.

Proof

By
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, all integer variables in
\Gamma
must be in
the domain of
\mathit{R}
and must be an integer. From
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma
, any free variables
in any refinement of any type in
\Gamma
must be an integer valued variable in
\Gamma
,
which gives the required result.

Definition 3

We will write
\mathit{R}\sqsubseteq\mathit{R^{\prime}}
to denote two register files such that:

1.

\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})\subseteq\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R^{\prime}})
, and

2.

\forall\,\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R}).\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})=\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x})

Definition 4

Two heaps
\mathit{H}
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}
are
equivalent modulo
\mathit{a}
, written
\mathit{H}\approx_{\mathit{a}}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
if:

1.

\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})=\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H^{\prime}})

2.

\forall\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathit{a^{\prime}}\,\neq\,\mathit{a}\implies\mathit{H}(\mathit{a})=\mathit{H}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})

3.

For any
n
,
\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{a}\Downarrow n
iff
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\vdash\mathit{a}\Downarrow n

Lemma 18

For any type
\tau=\top_{\mathit{n}}
,
\mathit{H},\mathit{v}
,
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\top_{\mathit{n}})=\emptyset
.

Proof

By induction on
\tau
. In the base case, the result is trivial.
Then consider the case where
\tau=\top_{{\mathit{n}-1}}\TREF^{0}
. If
\mathit{v}\not\in\textbf{Addr}
,
or if
\mathit{v}\,=\,\mathit{a}
and
\mathit{a}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})
, then the result trivially holds.
Otherwise the result holds from the inductive hypothesis, the definition of
+
and
\{\mathit{a}\mapsto 0\}
.

Lemma 19
(Heap Update Ownership Preservation)

If
\mathit{H}\approx_{\mathit{a}}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau)(\mathit{a})=0
, then

\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau)=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{v},\tau)
.

Proof

By induction on the shape of
\tau
. If
\tau=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}
then
the result trivially holds. Otherwise,
\tau=\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{r}
. We assume
that
\mathit{v}\,=\,\mathit{a^{\prime\prime}}
and
\mathit{a^{\prime\prime}}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})
(otherwise the result
trivially holds, as
\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})=\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H^{\prime}})
by
\mathit{H}\approx_{\mathit{a}}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
).
Consider the case where
\mathit{a^{\prime\prime}}\,=\,\mathit{a}
. By definition

\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{a},\tau)=\{\mathit{a}\mapsto r\}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{a}),\tau^{\prime})
, and by the
assumption that
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{a},\tau)(\mathit{a})=0
we must have that

r=0
. Further, by the ownership well-formedness of types,
we must have
\tau^{\prime}=\top_{\mathit{n}}
for some
n
, thus by
Lemma

18

we have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau)=\emptyset=\{\mathit{a}\mapsto 0\}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{H^{\prime}}(\mathit{a}),\top_{\mathit{n}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{v},\tau)
.

Finally, consider the case where
\mathit{a^{\prime\prime}}\,\neq\,\mathit{a}
. Then from the
definition of
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{a^{\prime\prime}},\tau)
and our assumption that

\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{a^{\prime\prime}},\tau)(\mathit{a})=0
, we have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{a^{\prime\prime}}),\tau^{\prime})(\mathit{a})=0
,
and the result holds from the inductive hypothesis and that
\mathit{H}(\mathit{a^{\prime\prime}})\,=\,\mathit{H^{\prime}}(\mathit{a^{\prime\prime}})
.

Lemma 20

For any
\mathit{H}
,
\mathit{R}
,
\mathit{v}
, and
\tau
, if
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
then
\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{v}\Downarrow|\tau|

Proof

By induction on
\tau
and the definition of
\mathbf{SATv}
.

Lemma 21

For any
n
, if
\mathit{H}\vdash v\Downarrow n
then for any
\mathit{R}
,
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\top_{\mathit{n}})
.

Proof

By induction on
n
. In the base case, by inversion on
\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{v}\Downarrow 0
we have

\mathit{v}\in\mathbb{Z}
and as
[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{v}/\nu]\top\implies\top
, we conclude
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\top_{{\mathrm{0}}})
.

For
n>0
, by inversion on
\mathit{H}\vdash v\Downarrow n
we have that

\mathit{v}\,=\,\mathit{a}
,
\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})
, and
\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{H}(\mathit{a})\Downarrow n-1
,
whereby the result holds from the inductive hypothesis.

Lemma 22
(Heap Update Consistency Preservation)

If
\mathit{H}\approx_{\mathit{a}}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau)(\mathit{a})=0
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
, then
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
.

Proof

By induction on the shape of
\tau
. The base case where

\tau=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}
is trivial. We therefore consider the case
where
\mathit{v}\,=\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and
\tau=\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{r}
.

We first consider the case where
\mathit{a^{\prime}}\,=\,\mathit{a}
, then by
our assumption that
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{a},\tau)(\mathit{a})=0
, we must have that

\tau=\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{0}
, whereby
\tau=\top_{\mathit{n}}
for some
\mathit{n}
.
From
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{a},\tau)
and
Lemma

20
, we must have that

\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{a}\Downarrow|\tau|
,
and from
\mathit{H}\approx_{\mathit{a}}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
, we therefore have that
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\vdash\mathit{a}\Downarrow|\tau|

whereby the result holds from
Lemma

21
.

Otherwise, we have that
\mathit{a^{\prime}}\,\neq\,\mathit{a}
, and by definition we must have that

\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{a}),\tau^{\prime})(\mathit{a})=0
and
\mathit{H^{\prime}}(\mathit{a})\,=\,\mathit{H}(\mathit{a})
hence the result follows
from the inductive hypothesis.

Lemma 23
(Register Weakening)

If
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)
and
\mathit{R}\vdash_{vs}\tau
,
then for any
\mathit{R^{\prime}}
such that
\mathit{R}\sqsubseteq\mathit{R^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},v,\tau)
.

Proof

By induction on the shape of
\tau
. If
\tau=\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{r}
, then
the result follows from the inductive hypothesis.
We therefore consider the case where

\tau=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}
. Without loss of generality, we consider
the case where
\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R^{\prime}})\setminus\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})=\left\{{x}\right\}
,
and
\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x})\,=\,n
. (If
\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x})\,=\,\mathit{a}
, the extra binding
also has no effect, and the case where more than one binding is
added follows from
n
applications of the following argument.)

From
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},v,\tau)
, we conclude that
v\in\mathbb{Z}
and that

[\mathit{R}]\,[v/\nu]\varphi
. If
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathbf{FPV}\,(\varphi)
then
[\mathit{R}]\,[v/\nu]\varphi\iff[\mathit{R^{\prime}}]\,[v/\nu]\varphi

and the result holds trivially. Otherwise, if
\mathit{x}\in\mathbf{FPV}\,(\varphi)
and
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})

then
\mathit{R}
is not a valid substitution, violating our assumption.

Lemma 24
(Heap Extension Consistency Preservation)

If we have heap
H
, such that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
, for any heap

\mathit{H^{\prime}}=\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\mapsto\mathit{v^{\prime}}\},\mathit{a}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})
, then we have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
.

Proof

By induction on the shape of
\tau
.
The base case where
\tau=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}
is trivial.
Next, we consider the case where
\tau=\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{r}
.
We must show that
\mathit{v}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H^{\prime}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{H^{\prime}}(\mathit{v}),\tau^{\prime})
.
The first condition is immediately satisfied by inversion on
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau^{\prime})
,
and from
\mathit{a}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})
, we have
v\neq a
, which gives that
H^{\prime}(v)=H(v)
.
That is we must show
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{H}(\mathit{v}),\tau^{\prime})
, which is follows from
the induction hypothesis.

Lemma 25
(Heap Extension Ownership Preservation)

If
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
, then for any
\mathit{a}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})

\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau)=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\mapsto\mathit{v^{\prime}}\},\mathit{v},\tau)
for any value
\mathit{v^{\prime}}
.

Proof

By induction on
\tau
. The base case is trivial as
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\})=\emptyset=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\mapsto\mathit{v}\},\mathit{v},\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\})
.
We therefore consider the case where
\tau=\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{r}
.

From
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau)
we must have that
\mathit{v}\,=\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and

\mathit{a^{\prime}}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})
(and by extension
\mathit{a^{\prime}}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\mapsto\mathit{v^{\prime}}\})
).
From the definition of the ownership function, we have that
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{v},\tau)=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{a}),\tau^{\prime})+\{\mathit{a^{\prime}}\mapsto r\}
.
and
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\mapsto\mathit{v^{\prime}}\},\mathit{v},\tau)=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\mapsto\mathit{v^{\prime}}\},\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\mapsto\mathit{v^{\prime}}\}(\mathit{a^{\prime}}),\tau^{\prime})+\{\mathit{a^{\prime}}\mapsto r\}

Then from our requirement that
\mathit{a}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})
, we have
\mathit{a}\,\neq\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}
and therefore

\mathit{H}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})\,=\,\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\mapsto\mathit{v^{\prime}}\}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})
, whereby the result holds from the inductive hypothesis.

Lemma 26
(Environment Weakening)

Define the partial operation
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
for two environments such
\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\cap\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})=\emptyset
:

(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\quad(\mathit{x})=\begin{cases}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\quad(\mathit{x})&\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\\
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\quad(\mathit{x})&\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\\
\textit{undef}&o.w.\end{cases}

Then, for any
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
where
\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)\cap\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset
:

1.

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
implies
\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}

2.

\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
implies
\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\leq\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

3.

If
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, and
\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, then
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\uplus\Gamma

Proof

1.

As in the proof of
Lemma

9
(part
3
), at the root of
the subtyping derivation is a logical judgment of the form

\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
which can be shown to be valid. We
must then show that
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
is valid. As

\left\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\uplus\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\left\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi
only strengthens the pre-condition

\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\uplus\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\wedge\varphi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\implies\varphi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
must
also be valid.

2.

It suffices to show that
\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash(\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{x})\leq(\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{x})
for
any arbitrary
\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
. If
\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
then we must have

\Gamma\vdash\Gamma(\mathit{x})\leq\Gamma^{\prime}(\mathit{x})
by inversion on
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
,
whereby
\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash(\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{x})=\Gamma(\mathit{x})\leq\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{x})=\Gamma^{\prime}(\mathit{x})
from part 1.

If
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
, then we must show
(\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{x})\leq\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{x})
, which trivially holds.

3.

By induction on the typing derivation of

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
. We assume that the variables bound in
any let expressions that appear within
\mathit{e}
are not in the domain
of
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
; this requirement can be easily enforced with
consistent renaming and is preserved during evaluation. The only interesting cases are

T-Sub
and
T-Assert
and the let bindings;
the other cases follow from the induction hypothesis.

We now prove the relevant cases.

Case
T-Let
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}\\
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{x}],\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{x}}\mathit{x}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{y}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}\\
\Gamma(\mathit{y})=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\quad\quad\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})\end{array}

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\uplus\Gamma[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{x}],\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{x}}\mathit{x}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{y}
.
To use the inductive hypothesis, we must show that

\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
. The latter holds by assumption.
To show the former, it suffices
to show
\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{x}
and
\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{x}}\mathit{x}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{y}
.
From the assumed well-formedness
\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, we must have
\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
,
and in particular
\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. From this
we conclude both conditions hold. To show the well-typing of the overall let expression,
we must show
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
, which follows from our assumption and
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
.
Finally, we must also show that
\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau
. From
\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau

and the fact that
\forall\,\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}).\Gamma^{\prime}(\mathit{x})=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\_}\right\}
iff

(\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{x})=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\_}\right\}
, we must have
\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau
.

Cases
T-LetInt
,
T-Mkref
,
T-Mkref
,
T-Deref
,
T-Call
:

Similar to the reasoning in
T-Let
.

Case
T-Sub
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\Gamma\leq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}&\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\\
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq\Gamma^{\prime},\tau\\
\end{array}

From the rules for subtyping, we must have

\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\subseteq\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)
and
\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})\subseteq\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})
. A simple inductive argument
gives that
\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\subseteq\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})
, therefore we have
\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})\subseteq\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
Let
\mathcal{LV}
be the set of free variables in the refinements of
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}

that are not in the domain of
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
. From the assumed well-formedness of

\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, we must have that

\forall x\in\mathcal{LV}.\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})\wedge\Gamma^{\prime}(\mathit{x})=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\_}\right\}
. Thus,

\mathcal{LV}\subseteq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\mathcal{LV}\subseteq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
. Further, by definition,
for any
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}\leq\Gamma_{\mathit{q}}
, if
\Gamma_{\mathit{q}}(\mathit{x})=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\_}\right\}
then
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{x})=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\_}\right\}
,
i.e. subtyping preserves simple types.
We conclude that
\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
and

\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, whereby the inductive hypothesis gives

\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
. To prove the overall result, we must
show that
\Gamma\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\leq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq\Gamma^{\prime}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\tau

which follow from parts 1 and 2 above.
That
\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\uplus\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
follows by reasoning to the case for
T-Let
above.

Case
T-Assert
:

We must show that
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\uplus\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\implies\varphi
which is equivalent to

\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\implies\varphi
. As the source term was well typed,

\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\implies\varphi
is valid, we must then have
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}\right\rrbracket\wedge\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\implies\varphi

whereby the inductive hypothesis gives the required result.

Appendix 0.C
Proof of
Lemma

3

We first prove two additional lemmas.

Lemmas

27
and
28
give key facts used in the return and
call cases respectively; we have separated them into separate lemmas for clarity.

Lemma 27

For any
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
such that
\Theta\mid\ell:\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Theta\mid[]:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{y}=[]^{\ell}\IN\mathit{e}]:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
then

\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{y}=[]^{\ell}\IN\mathit{e}][\mathit{x}]:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Proof

It suffices to show that
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\LET\mathit{y}=\mathit{x}\IN\mathit{e}:\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

and
\Theta\mid[]:\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}

\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

.

By inversion on

we have

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(1)

\displaystyle\Theta\mid[]:\tau^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid\mathcal{L}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(2)

\displaystyle\mathit{y}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})

(3)

\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

, and then

\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}
,
\tau_{\mathit{p}}
, and
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}
such:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\leq\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}

(4)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}},\tau_{\mathit{p}}\leq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(5)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}=\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}]

(6)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{x})=\tau_{\mathit{p}}+\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

(7)

We first apply

with

and

, so it remains to show

which, by

holds if we show that:

\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}\wedge_{\mathit{x}}\mathit{x}=_{\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}}\mathit{y}],\mathit{y}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}=_{\tau_{\mathit{p}}}\mathit{x}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(

\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}
,
\tau_{\mathit{p}}
and
\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}
and that
\mathit{x}
and

appear in the refinements
iff they are mapped to integer types in the new type environment.)

We can use

to weaken the type environment to:

\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}],\mathit{y}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}

From

above, we have that
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}],\mathit{y}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\leq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}
,
whereby one final application of
T-Sub
allows us to use to

Equation

1
above.

Lemma 28

Let
\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{f}^{\ell}(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}})\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}]
be a term such that:

Θ

_WF Θ

\Theta(\mathit{f})=\forall\lambda.\left\langle{\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}},\dots,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\tau_{\mathit{n}}}\right\rangle\ra\left\langle{\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\dots,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}\mid\tau_{\mathit{q}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\Theta\mid[]:\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}}\mid\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=[]^{\ell}\IN\mathit{e}]:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}

Proof
