on

\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{f}^{\ell}(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}})\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
we have, for some

\Gamma_{\mathit{p}},\tau_{\mathit{p}},\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}
, that:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\leq\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}

(9)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}},\tau_{\mathit{p}}\leq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(10)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}})=\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{i}}

(11)

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}

(12)

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}}\hookleftarrow\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}],\mathit{x}:\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{q}}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

(13)

\displaystyle\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}})

(14)

To prove the first part, from the well-typing of the function body, we have

\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ldots,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\tau_{\mathit{n}}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{\mathit{q}}\produces\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ldots,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}
.
From our assumption that all variable names are distinct,
by
n
applications of the substitution lemma (
Lemma

10
) we have:

\Theta\mid\lambda\mid\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{n}}\vdash\sigma_{x}\mathit{e}:\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{q}}\produces\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\sigma_{x}\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\sigma_{x}\,\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}
.
By
Lemma

9
(part
5
) we then have

\Theta\mid\ell:\vec{\ell}\mid\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{n}}\vdash\sigma_{x}\mathit{e}:\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{q}}\produces\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}
.
We take
\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}=\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau_{\mathit{q}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}}=\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{y_{\mathit{i}}}\hookleftarrow\sigma_{\alpha}\,\sigma_{x}\,\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}]
.

By the well-formedness of function types and well-formedness of
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}
,
we must have that
\ell:\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}}
.
Then by
Equations

11
,
12
and
26
we have

\Theta\mid\ell:\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}\vdash\sigma_{x}\mathit{e}:\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}}
, whereby
Equation

9

and an application of
T-Sub

gives
\Theta\mid\ell:\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\sigma_{x}\mathit{e}:\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}}
, i.e., the first result.

To prove the second part, from the typing rule for
TE-Stack
we must show:

\displaystyle\Theta\mid[]:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\mid\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(15)

\displaystyle\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})

(16)

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}},\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}

(17)

Equation

15
holds by assumption, and
Equation

16
follows from

Equation

14
and that
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}\leq\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
implies
\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}})\subseteq\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}})
.

\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}
follows from the well-typing of the function call term,
and
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}},\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{3}}}
follows from
Equation

13
.

From
Equations

10
and
13
we then have

Equation

17
via an application of
T-Sub
.

Proof
(Preservation;
Lemma

3
)

The proof is organized by cases analysis on the transition rule used of
\mathit{e}
, and showing that the output configuration is well typed by

\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}
. We must therefore find a
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
that is consistent with
\mathit{H^{\prime}}
and
\mathit{R^{\prime}}
and also satisfies the other conditions imposed by
the definition of
\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}
. Here
\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}
represent the type environment, heap and register after the transition respectively.
We identify the heap and register file before transition with
\mathit{H}
and
\mathit{R}
respectively.
In order to show that the ownership invariant is preserved, we need to prove that
\forall\,\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{a})\leq 1
.
In many cases, we will show that
\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)=\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
, whereby
from the assumption that
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
as implied by
\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}

we have
\forall\,\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)(\mathit{a})\leq 1
, giving the desired result.

Case
R-Var
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},F_{{\mathit{n}-1}}:\vec{F},\mathit{x}}\right\rangle,\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},F_{{\mathit{n}-1}}:\vec{F},\mathit{x}}\right\rangle\longrightarrow_{\mathit{D}}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},F_{{\mathit{n}-1}}[\mathit{x}]}\right\rangle\\
\end{array}

By inversion on configuration typing
\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},F_{{\mathit{n}-1}}:\vec{F},\mathit{x}}\right\rangle
, we have:

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{x}:\tau_{\mathit{n}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{n}}

\displaystyle\forall i\in\left\{{1..n}\right\}.\Theta\mid[]:\tau_{\mathit{i}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{i}}\mid\vec{\ell}_{{\mathit{i}-1}}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}F_{{\mathit{i}-1}}:\tau_{{\mathit{i}-1}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathit{i}-1}}

Using
Lemma

27
, we can conclude that
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}_{{\mathit{n}-1}}\mid\Gamma\vdash F_{{\mathit{n}-1}}[\mathit{x}]:\tau_{{\mathit{n}-1}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathit{n}-1}}
. We therefore take
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma
.

It remains to show that
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
which follows immediately from

\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
.

Case
R-Deref
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=*\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\
\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=*\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\longrightarrow_{\mathit{D}}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{\mathit{x^{\prime}}\mapsto\mathit{v}\},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\
\mathit{H}(\mathit{a})\,=\,\mathit{v}\quad\quad\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})\,=\,\mathit{a}\quad\quad\mathit{R^{\prime}}=\mathit{R}\{\mathit{x^{\prime}}\mapsto\mathit{v}\}\\
\end{array}

By inversion on the configuration typing relationship, we have that:

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=*\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e}]:\tau_{\mathit{n}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{n}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}})

By
Lemma

7
, we have some
\tau,\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
such that:

\displaystyle\Theta\mid[]:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}\mid\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E}:\tau_{\mathit{n}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{n}}

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\vdash\LET\mathit{x}=*\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}

Using
Lemma

6
, we have some
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}
and
\tau_{\mathit{p}}
such
that:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}\leq\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}\quad\quad\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}\quad\quad\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}},\tau_{\mathit{p}}\leq\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}},\tau

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y})=(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})\TREF^{r}\quad\quad\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}})

\displaystyle\tau^{\prime\prime}=\begin{cases}(\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\mathit{x})&r>0\\
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}&r=0\end{cases}

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime\prime}\TREF^{r}],\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

From
Lemma

12
, we then have
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{\mathit{p}})
.
We will now show that:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{\mathit{x^{\prime}}\mapsto\mathit{v}\},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})

(18)

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}:[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\,\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\,\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}

(19)

where
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow([\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\,\tau^{\prime\prime})\TREF^{r}],\mathit{x^{\prime}}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Together these give our desired result. To see how,
from
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime\prime}\TREF^{r}],\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}
above,
we must have that
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}
.
From
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}})
we must therefore have that

[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\,\tau_{\mathit{p}}=\tau_{\mathit{p}}
and
[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\,\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}}
. As
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathit{p}},\tau_{\mathit{p}}\leq\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}},\tau

an application of
T-Sub
gives

\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}
. Then
Lemma

8

will give that
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathit{E}[[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}]:\tau_{\mathit{n}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{n}}
.

As
\mathit{E}
and the stack
\vec{F}
remained unchanged, combined with

Equation

18
this gives

\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{\mathit{x^{\prime}}\mapsto\mathit{v}\},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle
as required.
As the above argument is used almost completely unchanged
in all of the following cases, we will invert the redex without regard
for the
T-Sub
rule, with the understanding that the subtyping rule
is handled with an argument identical to the above.

We now show that
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}:[\mathit{x}/\mathit{x^{\prime}}]\,\tau_{\mathit{p}}\produces[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\,\Gamma^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{\mathit{x^{\prime}}\mapsto\mathit{v}\},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
.
The first is easy to obtain using
Lemma

10
; from
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
,
we have that
\forall\,\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma).\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})
, whereby from
\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})
we have

\mathit{x^{\prime}}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)
.
It therefore remains to show
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{\mathit{x^{\prime}}\mapsto\mathit{v}\},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
.

To show
\mathbf{SAT}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
, it suffices to
show that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})

and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y})),\tau^{\prime\prime})
(that
\mathbf{SATv}
holds for all other variables

\mathit{z}
follows from
\Gamma(\mathit{z})=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{z})
and
Lemmas

17
and
23
).
If
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is an integer type and
r>0
, then
by the definition of the strengthening operator, the latter is equivalent to show
that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})

and that
\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}})=\mathit{H}(\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}))=\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}))
, which is immediate from the definition of
R-Deref
.
If
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}
is not an integer or if
r=0
,
then we must only show that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.

From
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{\mathit{p}})
, we know that
\mathbf{SAT}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{\mathit{p}})
, in particular,
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y}))
.
Then by
Lemmas

16
,
17
and
23
, from
\mathit{R}\sqsubseteq\mathit{R^{\prime}}
,
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}
, and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
we obtain
that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
, where
\mathit{v}\,=\,\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}))
.
We thus have that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
are satisfied.

We must also show that the ownership invariant is preserved.
Then, it’s to show
\forall\,\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{a})\leq 1
. Define
\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}
and
\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{\mathit{p}})

\displaystyle=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y}))

\displaystyle\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{y}))+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\mathit{z}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)\setminus\left\{{\mathit{y}}\right\}}\,\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{z}),\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{z}))

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\mathit{z}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\setminus\{\mathit{y},\mathit{x^{\prime}}\}}\,\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{z^{\prime}}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{z^{\prime}}))

By
Lemma

19
,
\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
holds. Then, it suffices to show that

\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y}))=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{y}))+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}))
.

As
\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}})=\mathit{H}(\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}))=\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}))
and from the definition of
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})

\displaystyle\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{y}))

\displaystyle=\{\mathit{a}\mapsto r\}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})

From the definition of the ownership function, we have that

\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y}))=\{\mathit{a}\mapsto r\}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})

which, by
Lemma

15
, is equivalent to:

\{\mathit{a}\mapsto r\}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})

We therefore have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma(\mathit{y}))=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{y}))+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}))
, and conclude that
\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)=\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
.

Case
R-Seq
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathit{x}\SEQ\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\

) we have for some
\Gamma

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma[\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}]\vdash\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}\produces\Gamma[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}]

.

)

and
16
.

Case

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\

) we have that for some

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma

)

To show that the output environment is consistent, we must show that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{x^{\prime}}}\mathit{x^{\prime}}\,=\,\mathit{y})

By reasoning similar to that in

, it suffices to show that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})

We know that
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, from which me have
\mathbf{SAT}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, in particular
\mathit{y}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma(\mathit{y}))
.
As
\mathit{R}\sqsubseteq\mathit{R^{\prime}}
and
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma
, from

16
,
17
and
23
, we obtain from
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
where
v=R(y)
.
We then have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
are satisfied.

We must also show that the ownership invariant is preserved.
Then, it’s to show
\forall\,\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{a})\leq 1
. Define
\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}
and
\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)

\displaystyle=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma(\mathit{y}))

\displaystyle\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{y}))+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\mathit{z}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)\setminus\left\{{\mathit{y}}\right\}}\,\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{z}),\Gamma(\mathit{z}))

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\mathit{z}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\setminus\{\mathit{y},\mathit{x^{\prime}}\}}\,\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{z^{\prime}}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{z^{\prime}}))

By
Lemma

19
,
\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
holds.
That
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
follows immediately
from

15
and the condition
\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})=\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}})=\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y})
.
We therefore conclude that
\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)=\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
.

Case
R-LetInt
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=n\IN\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\
\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=n\IN\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\longrightarrow_{\mathit{D}}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{\mathit{x^{\prime}}\mapsto n\},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\end{array}

By inversion (see
R-Deref
) we have that, for some
\Gamma
:

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,n}\right\}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)\quad\quad\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})

We give that
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma,\mathit{x^{\prime}}:\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\nu\,=\,n}\right\}
, and it thus suffices to show
that
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}

and
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}\{\mathit{x^{\prime}}\mapsto n\},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
.
The first one is easy to obtain using the
Lemma

10
(see
R-Deref
)
and the latter is trivial by similar reasoning to the
T-Let
and
T-Deref
cases.

Case
R-IfTrue
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\IFZERO\mathit{y}\THEN\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\ELSE\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]}\right\rangle\\
\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\IFZERO\mathit{y}\THEN\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\ELSE\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]}\right\rangle\longrightarrow_{\mathit{D}}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]}\right\rangle\end{array}

By inversion (see
R-Deref
) we have that for some
\Gamma
:

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathit{x})=\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi}\right\}

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\wedge\nu\,=\,0}\right\}]\vdash\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)

We take
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\left\{{\nu\COL\mathbf{int}\mid\varphi\wedge\nu\,=\,0}\right\}]
, and want to show that
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
(that
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
is immediate).

By definition, from
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
we have
\mathbf{SAT}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, in particular
\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})
,
\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\in\mathbb{Z}
and
[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})/\nu]\varphi
.
The refinement predicates
\varphi
still holds in the output environment, since nothing changes in the register after transition.
Also from precondition of
R-IfTrue
, we have
\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\,=\,0
, thus
\mathit{x}
satisfies the refinement that
\nu\,=\,0
.
Thus
[\mathit{R}]\,[\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})/\nu](\varphi\wedge\nu\,=\,0)
is trivially satisfied.

Case
R-IfFalse
:

Similar to the case for
R-IfTrue
.

Case
R-MkRef
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=\MKREF\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\
\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=\MKREF\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\longrightarrow_{\mathit{D}}\left\langle{\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\
\mathit{a}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})\quad\quad\mathit{x^{\prime}}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})\\
\mathit{H^{\prime}}=\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\mapsto\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})\}\quad\quad\mathit{R^{\prime}}=\mathit{R}\{\mathit{x^{\prime}}\mapsto\mathit{a}\}\end{array}

By inversion (see
R-Deref
) we have that for some
\Gamma
:

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathit{y})=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}],\mathit{x}:(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{x}}\mathit{x}\,=\,\mathit{y})\TREF^{1}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)\quad\quad\mathit{x}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})

We give
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma[\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}],\mathit{x^{\prime}}:(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{x^{\prime}}}\mathit{x^{\prime}}\,=\,\mathit{y})\TREF^{1}
,
and must show that
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash[\mathit{x^{\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
and

\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
. The first follows
from
Lemma

10
and the reasoning found in
R-Deref
,
and the second from the assumed well-formedness of
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

It remains to show
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
.
To show that the output environment is consistent, we must show that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{x^{\prime}}}\mathit{x^{\prime}}\,=\,\mathit{y})\TREF^{1})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
By reasoning similar to that in
R-Deref
, it suffices to show that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{1})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
We know that
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, from which we have
\mathbf{SAT}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, in particular
\mathit{y}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma(\mathit{y}))
.
As
\mathit{R}\sqsubseteq\mathit{R^{\prime}}
and
\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{WF}}\Gamma
, from
Lemmas

23
and
17
, we have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
implies
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.
By
Lemma

24
, we then have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.
Then by
Lemma

16
, we have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{v},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
where
\mathit{v}\,=\,\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})
.
We then have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{x^{\prime}}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{1})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
are satisfied.

We must also show that the ownership invariant is preserved.
Then, it’s to show
\forall\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{a^{\prime}})\leq 1
. From
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
and
Lemmas

25
and
15
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\mathit{z}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})}\,\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}}(\mathit{z}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{z}))

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\mathit{z}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)}\,\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{z}),\Gamma(\mathit{z}))+\{\mathit{a}\mapsto 1\}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)+\{\mathit{a}\mapsto 1\}

Since
\mathit{a}\not\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})
and
\forall\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)(\mathit{a^{\prime}})\leq 1
,
we have
\forall\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{a^{\prime}})\leq 1
.

Case
R-Assign
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathit{y}\WRITE\mathit{x}\SEQ\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\
\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathit{y}\WRITE\mathit{x}\SEQ\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\longrightarrow_{\mathit{D}}\left\langle{\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\
\mathit{a}\,=\,\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})\quad\quad\mathit{H^{\prime}}=\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\hookleftarrow\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\}\quad\quad\mathit{R^{\prime}}=\mathit{R}\end{array}

By inversion (see the
R-Deref
case) we have that

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma[\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}][\mathit{y}:\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{1}]\vdash\mathit{y}\WRITE\mathit{x}\SEQ\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}][\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{x})\TREF^{1}]\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)

We give
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}][\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}=_{\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mathit{x})\TREF^{1}]
. That

\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
is immediate.

We must therefore show that
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R^{\prime}},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
.
To show that the output environment is consistent, we must show that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),(\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\wedge_{\mathit{y}}\mathit{y}\,=\,\mathit{x})\TREF^{1})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
By reasoning similar to that in
R-Deref
, it suffices to show that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{1})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.

From
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, we know that
\mathbf{SAT}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, in particular,
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\Gamma(\mathit{x}))
.
If we show that
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})(\mathit{a})=0
and
\mathit{H}\approx_{\mathit{a}}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
,
then, by
Lemma

22
, we will obtain
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
,
from which, by
Lemma

16
,

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
follow.
We then have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{1})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
as
\mathit{H^{\prime}}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}))\,=\,\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})
.
(That any other variables
\mathit{z}
is consistent will follow from
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{z}),\Gamma(\mathit{z}))(\mathit{a})=0

as proved below and
lemma

22
.)

To show
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})(\mathit{a})=0
,
we define
\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}},\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and
\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
as below:

\displaystyle\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)

\displaystyle=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}+\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\mathit{z}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)\setminus\{\mathit{y},\mathit{x}\}}\,\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{z}),\Gamma(\mathit{z}))

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma(\mathit{y}))+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\Gamma(\mathit{x}))

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\mathit{z}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\setminus\{\mathit{y},\mathit{x}\}}\,\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{z}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{z}))

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{y}))+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{x}))

By the definition of the ownership function,
\Gamma(\mathit{y})=\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{1}
and
\Gamma(\mathit{x})=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau^{\prime})+\{\mathit{a}\mapsto 1\}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{H^{\prime}}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})+\{\mathit{a}\mapsto 1\}+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})

As
\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)(\mathit{a})\leq 1
(from
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
) and from

\displaystyle\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)(\mathit{a})

\displaystyle=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a})+\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a})

\displaystyle=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau^{\prime})(\mathit{a})+1+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})(\mathit{a})

\displaystyle=1

we have that:

\displaystyle\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau^{\prime})(\mathit{a})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})(\mathit{a})

\displaystyle=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a})=0

We now show that
\mathit{H}\approx_{\mathit{a}}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
. The first two conditions are clear, so it
remains to show that, for any
n
,
\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{a}\Downarrow n
iff
\mathit{H^{\prime}}\vdash\mathit{a}\Downarrow n
.
From
Lemma

20
, we have
\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{a}\Downarrow|\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{1}|
, and
a proof by contradiction gives that
|\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{1}|
is the only such
n

for which
\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{a}\Downarrow n
. We now argue the forward case for the bi-implication,
the backwards case follows similar reasoning.

Given
\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{a}\Downarrow|\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{1}|
, we must show
\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\hookleftarrow\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\}\vdash\mathit{a}\Downarrow|\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{1}|
, for which it suffices to show

\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\hookleftarrow\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\}\vdash\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\Downarrow|\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}|
.
From our requirement that
\tau^{\prime}
and
\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}

(and therefore
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}
) have similar shapes, we have
|\tau^{\prime}|=|\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}|=|\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}|
.
By inverting the well-typing of the input configuration, we must have
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
,
thus by
Lemma

20
we must have
\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\Downarrow|\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}|
.
As
|\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}|\,=\,|\tau^{\prime}|<|\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{1}|
,
\mathit{a}
cannot be reachable from
\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})
in
\mathit{H}

(otherwise we would have
\mathit{a}
reaches an integer along multiple heap paths of differing lengths,
a clear contradiction).
Then the value of
\mathit{a}
in
\mathit{H}
is irrelevant to the derivation of
\mathit{H}\vdash\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\Downarrow|\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}|
,
whereby
\mathit{H}\{\mathit{a}\hookleftarrow\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\}\vdash\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\Downarrow|\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}|
must hold.

Then, it’s to show
\forall\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H^{\prime}}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{a^{\prime}})=(\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}})(\mathit{a^{\prime}})=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})+\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})\,\leq\,1
.
For every
\mathit{z}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)\setminus\{\mathit{y},\mathit{x}\}
(and similarly for
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
), we have

\Gamma(\mathit{z})=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(\mathit{z})
. Further, from
\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a})=0
above, we must have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{z}),\Gamma(\mathit{z}))(\mathit{a})=0

for all such
\mathit{z}
. As
\mathit{H}\approx_{\mathit{a}}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
, by
Lemma

19
, we have that
\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
.
Then, from
\forall\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)(\mathit{a^{\prime}})=\mathit{O^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})+\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})\,\leq\,1
, it suffices to show
that
\forall\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})\leq\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})
.

We first consider the case for
\mathit{a}
:

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathit{a})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{H^{\prime}}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})(\mathit{a})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})(\mathit{a})+1

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})(\mathit{a})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau^{\prime})(\mathit{a})+1

From above, we have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})(\mathit{a})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau^{\prime})(\mathit{a})=0
.
By
Lemma

19
and
\mathit{H}\approx_{\mathit{a}}\mathit{H^{\prime}}
, we have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})
.
Also by
Lemma

15
, we have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.
From
\mathit{H^{\prime}}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}))\,=\,\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})
, we therefore have
\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{H^{\prime}}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})(\mathit{a})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})(\mathit{a})=0
,
and thus:

\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathit{a})=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{H^{\prime}}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})(\mathit{a})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})(\mathit{a})+1=1=\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a})

Next, consider some
\mathit{a}\,\neq\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}
;

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{H^{\prime}}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}})(\mathit{a^{\prime}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})(\mathit{a^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})(\mathit{a^{\prime}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})),\tau^{\prime})(\mathit{a^{\prime}})

By reasoning similar to the case for
a=a^{\prime}
, we have
\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})\leq\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}})(\mathit{a^{\prime}})\leq\mathit{O^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{0}}}}(\mathit{a^{\prime}})
.
We therefore conclude that
\forall\,\mathit{a^{\prime}}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H^{\prime}}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H^{\prime}},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{a^{\prime}})\leq 1
.

Case
R-Alias
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathbf{alias}(\mathit{x}=\mathit{y})\SEQ\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\
\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathbf{alias}(\mathit{x}=\mathit{y})\SEQ\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\longrightarrow_{\mathit{D}}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\
\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\,=\,\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})\end{array}

By inversion (see
R-Deref
) we have for some
\Gamma
that:

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma[\mathit{x}:\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}][\mathit{y}:\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\vdash\mathbf{alias}(\mathit{x}=\mathit{y})\SEQ\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}][\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\approx\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)

We give
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma[\mathit{x}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}][\mathit{y}\hookleftarrow\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]
, and must show

\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
.
The first is immediate.

To show
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
we first define:

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathit{p}\,{\mathrm{1}}}=

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathit{p}\,{\mathrm{2}}}=

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathit{q}\,{\mathrm{1}}}=

\displaystyle\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathit{q}\,{\mathrm{2}}}=

\displaystyle\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathit{q}}=

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathit{q}\,{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{\mathit{q}\,{\mathrm{2}}}

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathit{p}}=

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathit{p}\,{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{\mathit{p}\,{\mathrm{2}}}

We thus have
\tau_{\mathit{q}}\approx\tau_{\mathit{p}}
.

We know that
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, from which we have
\mathbf{SAT}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
, in particular

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\Gamma(\mathit{y})=\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\Gamma(\mathit{x})=\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}})
.
From
\tau_{\mathit{p}\,{\mathrm{1}}}+\tau_{\mathit{p}\,{\mathrm{2}}}=\tau_{\mathit{p}}
and
Lemma

16
, we have

\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{p}\,{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{p}\,{\mathrm{2}}})
imply
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{p}})
, where
\mathit{v}=\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}))=\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}))
.
From
\tau_{\mathit{q}}\approx\tau_{\mathit{p}}
and
Lemma

13
, we have that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{p}})
implies
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{q}})
.
From
Lemma

16
we also have that
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{q}})
implies
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{q}\,{\mathrm{1}}})
and
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{v},\tau_{\mathit{q}\,{\mathrm{2}}})
, where
again
\mathit{v}=\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}))=\mathit{H}(\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}))
.

Then from the reasoning above, the refinements of
\tau_{\mathit{q}\,{\mathrm{1}}}
and
\tau_{\mathit{q}\,{\mathrm{2}}}
are valid and
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
holds.

Then, it’s to show
\forall\,\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H}).\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})(\mathit{a})\leq 1
.
To prove that
\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)=\mathbf{Own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
follows from:

\displaystyle\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{x}),\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}})+\mathbf{own}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}})

which follows immediately from the conditions
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\approx\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\TREF^{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
,
\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\,=\,\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})
, and
Lemmas

15
and
14
.

Case
R-AliasPtr
:

By reasoning similar to the
R-Alias
case.

Case
R-AliasFail,R-AliasPtrFail
:

The result configuration
\mathbf{AliasFail}
is trivially well-typed.

Case
R-Assert
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathbf{assert}(\varphi)\SEQ\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\quad\quad\Gamma\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi\\
\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathbf{assert}(\varphi)\SEQ\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\longrightarrow_{\mathit{D}}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\
\end{array}

By inversion (see
R-Deref
) we can obtain
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{assert}(\varphi)\SEQ\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
, and
the result follows immediately by taking
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma
.

Case
R-AssertFail
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathbf{assert}(\varphi)\SEQ\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\\
\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\mathbf{assert}(\varphi)\SEQ\mathit{e}]}\right\rangle\longrightarrow_{\mathit{D}}\mathbf{AssertFail}\\
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathbf{assert}(\varphi)\SEQ\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}\end{array}

By inversion (see the
R-Deref
case) we have that

\Gamma\models\varphi
, i.e.,
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\implies\varphi
,
for some
\Gamma
such that
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
.
From
Lemma

11
we therefore have
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket
.
From the precondition of
R-AssertFail
we have that

\not\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi
. But from
\models\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket\implies\varphi
and
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\left\llbracket{\Gamma}\right\rrbracket

we can conclude that
\models[\mathit{R}]\,\varphi
, yielding a contradiction.
We therefore conclude that this case is impossible.

Case
R-Call
:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{f}^{\ell}(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}})\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}]}\right\rangle\\
\mathit{f}\mapsto(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,..\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}})\mathit{e}\in\mathit{D}\\
\begin{array}[]{l}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{f}^{\ell}(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}})\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}]}\right\rangle\\
\quad\longrightarrow_{\mathit{D}}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=[]^{\ell}\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}]:\vec{F},[\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\cdots[\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}]\mathit{e}}\right\rangle\end{array}\\
\end{array}

We must show that

\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=[]^{\ell}\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}]:\vec{F},[\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\cdots[\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}]\mathit{e}}\right\rangle

for some
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
.

By inversion on the configuration typing, we have that, for some
\Gamma
:

\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{f}^{\ell}(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}})\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}]:\tau_{\mathit{n}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{n}}.

By
Lemma

7
, we then have for some
\tau
, and
\Gamma^{\prime}
that:

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma\vdash\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{f}^{\ell}(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}})\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\Theta\mid[]:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}\mid\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E}:\tau_{\mathit{n}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{n}}

Taking
\tau_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\tau,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}=\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}=\Gamma,\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\Gamma_{\mathit{n}},\tau_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\tau_{\mathit{n}}
,
by
Lemma

28
we have, for some
\tau^{\prime\prime\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
:

\displaystyle\Theta\mid\ell:\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma\vdash\sigma_{x}\mathit{e}:\tau^{\prime\prime\prime}\produces\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\Theta\mid[]:\tau^{\prime\prime\prime}\produces\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\mid\vec{\ell}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=[]^{\ell}\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}]:\tau_{\mathit{n}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{n}}

where:

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}

\displaystyle=[\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}]\cdots[\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}}/\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}]

\displaystyle\Theta(\mathit{f})

\displaystyle=\forall\lambda.\left\langle{\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\tau_{\mathit{i}},\dots,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\tau_{\mathit{n}}}\right\rangle\ra\left\langle{\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\dots,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}\COL\tau^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}\mid\tau_{\mathit{p}}}\right\rangle

We therefore take
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma
.

We must also prove that
\forall i\in\left\{{1..n+1}\right\}.\Theta\mid[]:\tau_{\mathit{i}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{i}}\mid\vec{\ell}_{{\mathit{i}-1}}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E^{\prime}_{{\mathit{i}-1}}}:\tau_{{\mathit{i}-1}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathit{i}-1}}
where
\mathit{E^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}=\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=[]^{\ell}\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}]
and
\mathit{E^{\prime}_{\mathit{i}}}=\mathit{E_{\mathit{i}}}(0\leq i<n)
,
which can be divided into proving

\forall i\in\left\{{1..n}\right\}.\Theta\mid[]:\tau_{\mathit{i}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{i}}\mid\vec{\ell}_{{\mathit{i}-1}}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E^{\prime}_{{\mathit{i}-1}}}:\tau_{{\mathit{i}-1}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathit{i}-1}}
and
\Theta\mid[]:\tau_{n+1}\produces\Gamma_{n+1}\mid\vec{\ell}_{\mathit{n}}\vdash_{\mathit{ectx}}\mathit{E^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}:\tau_{\mathit{n}}\produces\Gamma_{\mathit{n}}
. The first follows by inversion on
\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{E}[\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{f}^{\ell}(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}})\IN\mathit{e^{\prime}}]}\right\rangle
.
To show the latter, we define
\Gamma_{n+1}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\tau_{n+1}=\tau^{\prime\prime\prime}
, whereby the
well-typing holds from the result of applying
Lemma

28
above.

Finally,
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
follows immediately from
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma)
and
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\Gamma
.

Appendix 0.D
Proof of Progress

We first state the standard decomposition lemma.

Lemma 29
(Decomposition)

For any term
\mathit{e}
, either
\mathit{e}=\mathit{x}
for some
\mathit{x}
or there exists some
\mathit{E}
and
\mathit{e^{\prime}}
where
\mathit{E}[\mathit{e^{\prime}}]=\mathit{e}
and one of the following
cases hold:

1.

\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\LET\mathit{x}=\MKREF\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}

2.

\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}

3.

\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\LET\mathit{x}=n\IN\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}

4.

\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\LET\mathit{x}=*\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}

5.

\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{f}^{\ell}(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}})\IN\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}

6.

\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\mathit{x}\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}

7.

\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\mathbf{alias}(\mathit{x}=\mathit{y})\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}

8.

\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\mathbf{alias}(\mathit{x}=*\mathit{y})\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}

9.

\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\IFZERO\mathit{x}\THEN\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\ELSE\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

10.

\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\mathbf{assert}(\varphi)\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}

11.

\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\mathit{x}\WRITE\mathit{y}\SEQ\mathit{e}

Proof

Straightforward induction on
\mathit{e}
.

Proof
(Progress;
Lemma

4
)

By inversion on
\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\mathbf{C}
, either
\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{AliasFail}
or
\mathbf{C}=\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},\mathit{e}}\right\rangle
.
In the former case the result is immediate. In the latter case we have
that
\Theta\mid\vec{\ell}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e}:\tau\produces\Gamma^{\prime}
for some
\tau,\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
and further from
Lemma

29
, we have that either
\mathit{e}=\mathit{x}
for some
\mathit{x}
or there exists
some
\mathit{E}
or
\mathit{e^{\prime}}
where
\mathit{e}=\mathit{E}[\mathit{e^{\prime}}]
and
\mathit{e^{\prime}}
meets one of the cases in

Lemma

29
.

In the case
\mathit{e}=\mathit{x}
, we further make case analysis on the form of
\vec{F}
.
The case where
\vec{F}=\epsilon
is immediate;
In the other case where
\vec{F}=F:\vec{F}^{\prime}
, the configuration can step
to
\left\langle{\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\vec{F},F[\mathit{x}]}\right\rangle
according to
R-Var
.

For the remaining cases where
\mathit{e}=\mathit{E}[\mathit{e^{\prime}}]
,
by the well-typing of
\mathit{e}
with respect to
\Gamma
and
Lemma

7
,
we have that
\Theta\mid\mathcal{L}\mid\Gamma\vdash\mathit{e^{\prime}}:\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}\produces\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
some
\tau_{{\mathrm{0}}}
and
\Gamma_{{\mathrm{0}}}
.

We now treat the remaining forms of
\mathit{e^{\prime}}

Case:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\LET\mathit{x}=*\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}\end{array}

By inversion (
Lemma

6
) and
Lemma

12
we must have
that for some
\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}
where
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{\mathit{p}})
that
\mathit{y}\in\mathit{dom}(\Gamma_{\mathit{p}})
and

\Gamma_{\mathit{p}}(\mathit{y})=\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{r}
. From
\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{\mathit{p}})
we must have
\mathit{y}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})

and further
\mathbf{SATv}(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\mathit{R}(\mathit{y}),\tau^{\prime}\TREF^{r^{\prime}})
, from which we must have

\mathit{R}(\mathit{y})\,=\,\mathit{a}
and
\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})
. Then
\mathbf{C}
can step according to
R-Deref
.

Case:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}\end{array}

Again, by
Lemmas

6
and
12
and the definition
of
\mathbf{Cons}
, we must have that
\mathit{y}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})
, and the system can step
according to
R-LetVar
.

Case:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\LET\mathit{x}=\MKREF\mathit{y}\IN\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}\end{array}

Similar to the
R-LetVar
case above.

Case:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\LET\mathit{x}=n\IN\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}\\
\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\mathit{x}\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}\\
\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\mathbf{assert}(\varphi)\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}\end{array}

The first two can trivially step according to
R-LetInt
and
R-Seq
respectively.
the last can step according to
R-Assert
or
R-AssertFalse
(although
by
Lemmas

3
and
2
the latter is impossible).

Case:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\mathbf{alias}(\mathit{x}=\mathit{y})\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}\end{array}

Again by
Lemmas

6
and
12
and that

\mathbf{Cons}\,(\mathit{H},\mathit{R},\Gamma_{\mathit{p}})
implies
\mathit{x}
and
\mathit{y}
are bound to addresses
in the register file, we have that the configuration can step according to
R-Alias

or
R-AliasFail
.

Case:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\mathbf{alias}(\mathit{x}=*\mathit{y})\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}\end{array}

Similar to the case above, we must have that
\mathit{x}
is bound to an address
in the register file, and that
\mathit{y}
is bound to an address that
is itself mapped to an address in the heap
\mathit{H}
. Then the configuration may
step according to
R-AliasPtr
or
R-AliasPtrFail

Case:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e}=\IFZERO\mathit{x}\THEN\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\ELSE\mathit{e_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\end{array}

As above, from the well-typing we must have that
\mathit{x}
is bound in
\mathit{R}

to some integer
n
. Then the configuration may step according to
R-IfTrue

or
R-IfFalse
depending on whether

n\,=\,0
or
n\,\neq\,0
.

Case:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\mathit{x}\WRITE\mathit{y}\SEQ\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}\end{array}

From the well-typing of
\mathit{e^{\prime}}
,

Lemmas

6
and
12
and the definition
of
\mathbf{Cons}
, we must have that
\mathit{y}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})
,

\mathit{x}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{R})
,
\mathit{R}(\mathit{x})\,=\,\mathit{a}
, and
\mathit{a}\in\mathit{dom}(\mathit{H})
. Then
the configuration can step according
R-Assign
.

Case:

\quad\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l@{\qquad}l}\mathit{e^{\prime}}=\LET\mathit{x}=\mathit{f}^{\ell}(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{y_{\mathit{n}}})\IN\mathit{e^{\prime\prime}}\end{array}

From the well-typing of the function call we must have that
\mathit{f}\in\mathit{dom}(\Theta)
.
From
\Theta\vdash\mathit{D}
in the precondition of
\vdash_{\mathit{conf}}^{D}\mathbf{C}
, we must have that

\mathit{f}\mapsto(\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,..\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{j}}})\mathit{e^{\prime\prime\prime}}\in\mathit{D}
. Then from
T-FunDef
we must have that

j=n
whereby the configuration can step according to
R-Call
.
